<div id="gltf-2-0" class="section">

# glTF 2.0<a href="#gltf-2-0" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

<div id="id1" class="refbox admonition">

Reference

Category<span class="colon">:</span>  
Import-Export

Menu<span class="colon">:</span>  
<span class="menuselection">File ‣ Import/Export ‣ glTF 2.0 (.glb,
.gltf)</span>

</div>

<div id="enabling-add-on" class="section">

## Enabling Add-on<a href="#enabling-add-on" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

This add-on is enabled by default, in case it is not:

1.  Open Blender and go to
    <a href="../../editors/preferences/addons.html"
    class="reference internal"><span class="doc">Add-ons</span></a>
    section of the <a href="../../editors/preferences/index.html"
    class="reference internal"><span class="doc">Preferences</span></a>.

2.  Search “glTF 2.0” and check the *Enable Add-on* checkbox.

</div>

<div id="usage" class="section">

## Usage<a href="#usage" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

glTF™ (GL Transmission Format) is used for transmission and loading of
3D models in web and native applications. glTF reduces the size of 3D
models and the runtime processing needed to unpack and render those
models. This format is commonly used on the web, and has support in
various 3D engines such as Unity3D, Unreal Engine 4, and Godot.

This importer/exporter supports the following glTF 2.0 features:

- Meshes

- Materials (Principled BSDF) and Shadeless (Unlit)

- Textures

- Cameras

- Punctual lights (point, spot, and directional)

- Extensions (listed below)

- Extras (custom properties)

- Animation (keyframe, shape key, and skinning)

</div>

<div id="meshes" class="section">

## Meshes<a href="#meshes" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

glTF’s internal structure mimics the memory buffers commonly used by
graphics chips when rendering in real-time, such that assets can be
delivered to desktop, web, or mobile clients and be promptly displayed
with minimal processing. As a result, quads and n-gons are automatically
converted to triangles when exporting to glTF. Discontinuous UVs and
flat-shaded edges may result in moderately higher vertex counts in glTF
compared to Blender, as such vertices are separated for export.
Likewise, curves and other non-mesh data are not preserved, and must be
converted to meshes prior to export.

<div id="gpu-instances" class="section">

### GPU Instances<a href="#gpu-instances" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

When the option is enabled in Exporter, instances are exported using the
<span class="pre">`EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing`</span> extension. There are
some limitations, at export:

- Instances must be meshes, and don’t have any children themselves

- Instances must all be children of the same object.

- This extension doesn’t manage material variation. That means that the
  generated file may include all instances with same materials.

- Instances detected are objects sharing the same mesh data.

At import, instances are created by creating objects sharing the same
mesh data.

</div>

</div>

<div id="materials" class="section">

## Materials<a href="#materials" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

The core material system in glTF supports a metal/rough
<span class="abbr" title="Physically Based Rendering">PBR</span>
workflow with the following channels of information:

- Base Color

- Metallic

- Roughness

- Baked Ambient Occlusion

- Normal Map (tangent space, +Y up)

- Emissive

Some additional material properties or types of materials can be
expressed using glTF extensions. The complete list can be found in
<a href="#extensions" class="reference internal">Extensions</a> part of
this documentation.

<figure id="id4" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-channels.jpg"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-channels.jpg" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">An example of the various
image maps available in the glTF 2.0 core format. This is the <a
href="https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Sample-Models/tree/main/2.0/WaterBottle"
class="reference external">water bottle sample model</a> shown alongside
slices of its various image maps.</span><a href="#id4"
class="headerlink" title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

<div id="imported-materials" class="section">

### Imported Materials<a href="#imported-materials" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

The glTF material system is different from Blender’s own materials. When
a glTF file is imported, the add-on will construct a set of Blender
nodes to replicate each glTF material as closely as possible.

The importer supports Metal/Rough PBR (core glTF), Spec/Gloss PBR
(<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_pbrSpecularGlossiness`</span>) and
some extension materials. The complete list can be found in
<a href="#extensions" class="reference internal">Extensions</a> part of
this documentation.

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

Examining the result of the material import process is a good way to see
examples of the types of material nodes and settings that can be
exported to glTF.

</div>

</div>

<div id="exported-materials" class="section">

### Exported Materials<a href="#exported-materials" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

The exporter supports Metal/Rough PBR (core glTF) and Shadeless
(<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_unlit`</span>) materials. It will
construct a glTF material based on the nodes it recognizes in the
Blender material. The material export process handles the settings
described below.

<div class="admonition note">

Note

When image textures are used by materials, glTF requires that images be
in PNG or JPEG format. The add-on will automatically convert images from
other formats, increasing export time.

</div>

<div id="base-color" class="section">

#### Base Color<a href="#base-color" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

The glTF base color is determined by looking for a Base Color input on a
Principled BSDF node. If the input is unconnected, the input’s default
color (the color field next to the unconnected socket) is used as the
Base Color for the glTF material.

<figure id="id5" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-base-color-solid-green.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-base-color-solid-green.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">A solid base color can be
specified directly on the node.</span><a href="#id5" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

If an Image Texture node is found to be connected to the Base Color
input, that image will be used as the glTF base color.

<figure id="id6" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-base-color-image-hookup.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-base-color-image-hookup.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">An image is used as the glTF
base color.</span><a href="#id6" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

If no texture is connected, the base color can be specified:

- From the Principled BSDF node’s *Base Color* input, which is the
  default.

- From a RGB node connected to the *Base Color* input.

- From an Ambient Occlusion node connected to the *Base Color* input.
  (The AO socket is not used in glTF, but the color output can be used
  as a base color.)

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-base-color.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-base-color.png" />
</figure>

</div>

<div id="metallic-and-roughness" class="section">

#### Metallic and Roughness<a href="#metallic-and-roughness" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

These values are read from the Principled BSDF node. If both of these
inputs are unconnected, the node will display sliders to control their
respective values between 0.0 and 1.0, and these values will be copied
into the glTF.

When using an image, glTF expects the metallic values to be encoded in
the blue (<span class="pre">`B`</span>) channel, and roughness to be
encoded in the green (<span class="pre">`G`</span>) channel of the same
image. If images are connected to the Blender node in a manner that does
not follow this convention, the add-on may attempt to adapt the image to
the correct form during exporting (with an increased export time).

In the Blender node tree, it is recommended to use a Separate RGB node
to separate the channels from an Image Texture node, and connect the
green (<span class="pre">`G`</span>) channel to Roughness, and blue
(<span class="pre">`B`</span>) to Metallic. The glTF exporter will
recognize this arrangement as matching the glTF standard, and that will
allow it to simply copy the image texture into the glTF file during
export.

The Image Texture node for this should have its *Color Space* set to
Non-Color.

<figure id="id7" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-metal-rough.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-metal-rough.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">A metallic/roughness image
connected in a manner consistent with the glTF standard, allowing it to
be used verbatim inside an exported glTF file.</span><a href="#id7"
class="headerlink" title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

</div>

<div id="baked-ambient-occlusion" class="section">

#### Baked Ambient Occlusion<a href="#baked-ambient-occlusion" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

glTF is capable of storing a baked ambient occlusion map. Currently
there is no arrangement of nodes that causes Blender to use such a map
in exactly the same way as intended in glTF. However, if the exporter
finds a custom node group by the name of
<span class="pre">`glTF`</span>` `<span class="pre">`Material`</span>` `<span class="pre">`Output`</span>,
and finds an input named <span class="pre">`Occlusion`</span> on that
node group, it will look for an Image Texture attached there to use as
the occlusion map in glTF. The effect need not be shown in Blender, as
Blender has other ways of showing ambient occlusion, but this method
will allow the exporter to write an occlusion image to the glTF. This
can be useful to real-time glTF viewers, particularly on platforms where
there may not be spare power for computing such things at render time.

<figure id="id8" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-occlusion-only.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-occlusion-only.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">A pre-baked ambient occlusion
map, connected to a node that doesn’t render but will export to
glTF.</span><a href="#id8" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

If you enable Shader Editor Add-ons in preferences, you will be able to
add this custom node group from Menu: Add \> Output \> glTF Material
Output

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_addon-preferences-shader.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_addon-preferences-shader.png" />
</figure>

</div>

glTF stores occlusion in the red (<span class="pre">`R`</span>) channel,
allowing it to optionally share the same image with the roughness and
metallic channels.

<figure id="id9" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-orm-hookup.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-orm-hookup.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">This combination of nodes
mimics the way glTF packs occlusion, roughness, and metallic values into
a single image.</span><a href="#id9" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

The Cycles render engine has a Bake panel that can be used to bake
ambient occlusion maps. The resulting image can be saved and connected
directly to the
<span class="pre">`glTF`</span>` `<span class="pre">`Material`</span>` `<span class="pre">`Output`</span>
node.

</div>

</div>

<div id="normal-map" class="section">

#### Normal Map<a href="#normal-map" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

To use a normal map in glTF, connect an Image Texture node’s color
output to a Normal Map node’s color input, and then connect the Normal
Map normal output to the Principled BSDF node’s normal input. The Image
Texture node for this should have its *Color Space* property set to
Non-Color.

The Normal Map node must remain on its default property of Tangent Space
as this is the only type of normal map currently supported by glTF. The
strength of the normal map can be adjusted on this node. The exporter is
not exporting these nodes directly, but will use them to locate the
correct image and will copy the strength setting into the glTF.

<figure id="id10" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-normal.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-normal.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">A normal map image connected
such that the exporter will find it and copy it to the glTF
file.</span><a href="#id10" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

The Cycles render engine has a Bake panel that can be used to bake
tangent-space normal maps from almost any other arrangement of normal
vector nodes. Switch the Bake type to Normal. Keep the default space
settings (space: Tangent, R: +X, G: +Y, B: +Z) when using this bake
panel for glTF. The resulting baked image can be saved and plugged into
to a new material using the Normal Map node as described above, allowing
it to export correctly.

See: <a href="../../render/cycles/baking.html"
class="reference internal"><span class="doc">Cycles Render
Baking</span></a>

</div>

</div>

<div id="emissive" class="section">

#### Emissive<a href="#emissive" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

An Image Texture node can be connected to the Emission input on the
Principled BSDF node to include an emissive map with the glTF material.
Alternatively, the Image Texture node can be connected to an Emission
shader node, and optionally combined with properties from a Principled
BSDF node by way of an Add Shader node.

If the emissive map is alone in the material, it is best to set the Base
Color default to black, and the Roughness default to 1.0. This minimizes
the influence of the other channels if they are not needed.

<figure id="id11" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-emissive.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-emissive.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">This arrangement is supported
for backwards compatibility. It is simpler to use the Principled BSDF
node directly.</span><a href="#id11" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

If any component of emissiveFactor is \> 1.0,
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_emissive_strength`</span> extension
will be used.

</div>

<div id="clearcoat" class="section">

#### Clearcoat<a href="#clearcoat" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

When the *Clearcoat* input on the Principled BSDF node has a nonzero
default value or Image Texture node connected, the
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_clearcoat`</span> glTF extension will
be included in the export. This extension will also include a value or
Image Texture from the *Clearcoat Roughness* input if available.

If Image Textures are used, glTF requires that the clearcoat values be
written to the red (<span class="pre">`R`</span>) channel, and
*Clearcoat Roughness* to the green (<span class="pre">`G`</span>)
channel. If monochrome images are connected, the exporter will remap
them to these color channels.

The *Clearcoat Normal* input accepts the same kinds of inputs as the
base Normal input, specifically a tangent-space normal map with +Y up,
and a user-defined strength. This input can reuse the same normal map
that the base material is using, or can be assigned its own normal map,
or can be left disconnected for a smooth coating.

All Image Texture nodes used for clearcoat shading should have their
*Color Space* set to Non-Color.

<figure id="id12" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-clearcoat.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-clearcoat.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">An example of a complex
clearcoat application that will export correctly to glTF. A much
simpler, smooth coating can be applied from just the Principled BSDF
node alone.</span><a href="#id12" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

</div>

<div id="sheen" class="section">

#### Sheen<a href="#sheen" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

If a Sheen Roughness Texture is used, glTF requires the values be
written to the alpha (<span class="pre">`A`</span>) channel.

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-sheen.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-sheen.png" />
</figure>

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

Sheen BSDF node is only available on Cycles render engine. You may have
to temporary switch to Cycles to add this node, and get back to EEVEE.

</div>

</div>

<div id="specular" class="section">

#### Specular<a href="#specular" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

When the *Specular IOR Level* or *Specular Tint* input of Principled
BSDF node have a non default value or Image Texture node connected, the
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_specular`</span> glTF extension will be
included in the export.

</div>

<div id="anisotropy" class="section">

#### Anisotropy<a href="#anisotropy" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Anisotropic textures and data need to be converted at export, and at
import.

At import, some nodes are created to manage this conversion

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material_anisotropy.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material_anisotropy.png" />
</figure>

At export, this exact same nodes are detected, and used to export data.

At export, you can also plug some grayscale textures for *Anisotropic*
and *Anisotropic Rotation* sockets. Then, exporter will convert these
texture into a glTF compatible texture.

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material_anisotropy-grayscale-texture.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material_anisotropy-grayscale-texture.png" />
</figure>

Note that the *tangent* socket must be linked to a *tangent* node, with
UVMap. The chosen UVMap must be the UVMap of the Normal Map.

</div>

<div id="transmission" class="section">

#### Transmission<a href="#transmission" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

When the Transmission input on the Principled BSDF node has a nonzero
default value or Image Texture node connected, the
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_transmission`</span> glTF extension
will be included in the export. When a texture is used, glTF stores the
values in the red (<span class="pre">`R`</span>) channel. The *Color
Space* should be set to Non-Color.

Transmission is different from alpha blending, because transmission
allows full-strength specular reflections. In glTF, alpha blending is
intended to represent physical materials that are partially missing from
the specified geometry, such as medical gauze wrap. Transmission is
intended to represent physical materials that are solid but allow
non-specularly-reflected light to transmit through the material, like
glass.

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

The material’s base roughness can be used to blur the transmission, like
frosted glass.

</div>

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

Typically the alpha blend mode of a transmissive material should remain
“Opaque”, the default setting, unless the material only partially covers
the specified geometry.

</div>

<div class="admonition note">

Note

In real-time engines where transmission is supported, various technical
limitations in the engine may determine which parts of the scene are
visible through the transmissive surface. In particular, transmissive
materials may not be visible behind other transmissive materials. These
limitations affect physically-based transmission, but not alpha-blended
non-transmissive materials.

</div>

<div class="admonition note">

Note

If you want to enable refraction on your model,
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_transmission`</span> must also be used
in addition with <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_volume`</span>. See
the dedicated *Volume* part of the documentation.

</div>

<div class="admonition warning">

Warning

Transmission is complex for real-time rendering engines to implement,
and support for the
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_transmission`</span> glTF extension is
not yet widespread.

</div>

</div>

<div id="ior" class="section">

#### IOR<a href="#ior" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

At import, there are two different situation:

- if <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_ior`</span> is not set, IOR value
  of Principled BSDF node is set to 1.5, that is the glTF default value
  of IOR.

- If set, the <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_ior`</span> is used to
  set the IOR value of Principled BSDF.

At export, IOR is included in the export only if one of these extensions
are also used:

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_transmission`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_volume`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_specular`</span>

IOR of 1.5 are not included in the export, because this is the default
glTF IOR value.

</div>

<div id="volume" class="section">

#### Volume<a href="#volume" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Volume can be exported using a Volume Absorption node, linked to Volume
socket of Output node. Data will be exported using the
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_volume`</span> extension.

- For volume to be exported, some *transmission* must be set on
  Principled BSDF node.

- Color of Volume Absorption node is used as glTF attenuation color. No
  texture is allowed for this property.

- Density of Volume Absorption node is used as inverse of glTF
  attenuation distance.

- Thickness can be plugged into the Thickness socket of custom group
  node
  <span class="pre">`glTF`</span>` `<span class="pre">`Material`</span>` `<span class="pre">`Output`</span>.

- If a texture is used for thickness, it must be plugged on
  (<span class="pre">`G`</span>) Green channel of the image.

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-volume.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-volume.png" />
</figure>

</div>

<div id="gltf-variants" class="section">

#### glTF Variants<a href="#gltf-variants" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

<div class="admonition note">

Note

For a full Variants experience, you have to enable UI in Add-on
preferences

> <div>
>
> <figure class="align-default">
> <img
> src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_addon-preferences-variant.png"
> alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_addon-preferences-variant.png" />
> </figure>
>
> </div>

</div>

There are two location to manage glTF Variants in Blender

- In 3D View, on
  <span class="pre">`glTF`</span>` `<span class="pre">`Variants`</span>
  tab

- For advanced settings, in Mesh Material Properties (see Advanced glTF
  Variant checks)

The main concept to understand for using Variants, is that each material
slot will be used as equivalent of a glTF primitive.

</div>

<div id="gltf-variants-switching" class="section">

#### glTF Variants switching<a href="#gltf-variants-switching" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

After importing a glTF file including
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_variants`</span> extension, all
variants can be displayed.

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material_variants-switch.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material_variants-switch.png" />
</figure>

You can switch Variant, by *selecting* the variant you want to display,
then clicking on *Display Variant*.

You can switch to default materials (when no Variant are used), by
clicking on *Reset to default*.

</div>

<div id="gltf-variants-creation" class="section">

#### glTF Variants creation<a href="#gltf-variants-creation" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

You can add a new Variant by clicking <span class="bl-icons-add"></span>
at right of the Variant list. Then you can change the name by
double-clicking.

After changing Materials in Material Slots, you can assign current
materials to the active Variant using *Assign to Variant*.

You can also set default materials using *Assign as Original*. These
materials will be exported as default material in glTF. This are
materials that will be displayed by any viewer that don’t manage
<span class="pre">`KHR_materials_variants`</span> extension.

</div>

<div id="advanced-gltf-variant-checks" class="section">

#### Advanced glTF Variant checks<a href="#advanced-gltf-variant-checks" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

If you want to check primitive by primitive, what are Variants used, you
can go to Mesh Material Properties.

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material_variants-detail.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material_variants-detail.png" />
</figure>

The *glTF Material Variants* tab refers to the active material Slot and
Material used by this slot. You can see every Variants that are using
this material for the given Slot/Primitive.

You can also assign material to Variants from this tab, but
recommendation is to perform it from 3D View tab.

</div>

<div id="double-sided-backface-culling" class="section">

#### Double-Sided / Backface Culling<a href="#double-sided-backface-culling" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

For materials where only the front faces will be visible, turn on
*Backface Culling* (Camera) in the *Settings* panel of an EEVEE
material. When using other engines (Cycles, Workbench) you can
temporarily switch to EEVEE to configure this setting, then switch back.

Leave this box unchecked for double-sided materials.

<figure id="id13" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-backface-culling.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-backface-culling.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">The inverse of this setting
controls glTF’s <span class="pre"><code
class="docutils literal notranslate">DoubleSided</code></span>
flag.</span><a href="#id13" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

</div>

<div id="alpha-modes" class="section">

#### Alpha Modes<a href="#alpha-modes" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

glTF has three alpha modes, depending on whether the alpha value is
always 1, always 0 or 1, or can be between 0 and 1. The exporter
determines the alpha mode automatically from the nodes connected to the
Alpha socket.

Opaque  
> <div>
>
> In *opaque mode*, the material alpha is always 1.
>
> </div>

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-opaque.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-opaque.png" />
</figure>

Mask  
In *mask mode*, the material alpha is always 0 or 1. This creates
“cutout” transparency, where there is a hard edge between opaque and
transparent regions, and can be used for things like leaves or cloth
with holes. To enable this mode, use a Math node to round the alpha
value to either 0 or 1.

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-round-alpha.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-round-alpha.png" />
</figure>

Rounding snaps alpha values that are 0.5 or greater up to 1, and ones
below 0.5 down to 1. It is also possible to use a cutoff value different
than 0.5 by using Math nodes to do
<span class="pre">`1`</span>` `<span class="pre">`-`</span>` `<span class="pre">`(alpha`</span>` `<span class="pre">`<`</span>` `<span class="pre">`cutoff)`</span>.

Mask mode is essentially the same as EEVEE’s “Alpha Clip” blend mode,
but is done with shader nodes so it works in other render engines.

Blend  
Materials that use neither of these will use *blend mode*. Blend mode
allows partially transparent surfaces that tint the objects seen through
them, like layers of colored film. However, partial transparency is
complex to render, and glTF viewers may show visual artifacts in
non-trivial scenes that use blend mode.

To avoid artifacts, it may be a good idea to separate out parts of a
model that can use opaque or mask mode, and use blend mode only on the
parts where it is necessary, or to use only a *single* layer of
transparent polygons in front of opaque objects.

</div>

<div id="uv-mapping" class="section">

#### UV Mapping<a href="#uv-mapping" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Control over UV map selection and transformations is available by
connecting a UV Map node and a Mapping node to any Image Texture node.

Settings from the Mapping node are exported using a glTF extension named
<span class="pre">`KHR_texture_transform`</span>. There is a mapping
type selector across the top. *Point* is the recommended type for
export. *Texture* and *Vector* are also supported. The supported offsets
are:

- *Location* - X and Y

- *Rotation* - Z only

- *Scale* - X and Y

For the *Texture* type, *Scale* X and Y must be equal (uniform scaling).

<figure id="id14" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-mapping.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-mapping.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">A deliberate choice of UV
mapping.</span><a href="#id14" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

These nodes are optional. Not all glTF readers support multiple UV maps
or texture transforms.

</div>

</div>

<div id="factors" class="section">

#### Factors<a href="#factors" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Any Image Texture nodes may optionally be multiplied with a constant
color or scalar. These will be written as factors in the glTF file,
which are numbers that are multiplied with the specified image textures.
These are not common.

- Use Math node (multiply) for scalar factors. Use second value as
  factor

- Use Mix node (color / multiply) for color factors. Set Factor to 1,
  and use Color2 (B) as factors

<figure class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-factors.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-factors.png" />
</figure>

</div>

<div id="example" class="section">

#### Example<a href="#example" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

A single material may use all of the above at the same time, if desired.
This figure shows a typical node structure when several of the above
options are applied at once:

<figure id="id15" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-principled.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-principled.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">A Principled BSDF material
with an emissive texture.</span><a href="#id15" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

</div>

<div id="udim" class="section">

#### UDIM<a href="#udim" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

UDIM is a way to store multiple textures in a single image file. The
UDIM system is supported by Blender, but is not supported by glTF. When
exporting a model that uses UDIM, the add-on will automatically split
the image into multiple images, one for each tile, and will update the
material nodes to use the new images. All UDIM texture must use the same
UV map to be exported.

</div>

</div>

<div id="exporting-a-shadeless-unlit-material" class="section">

### Exporting a Shadeless (Unlit) Material<a href="#exporting-a-shadeless-unlit-material" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

To export an unlit material, mix in a camera ray, and avoid using the
Principled BSDF node.

<figure id="id16" class="align-default">
<img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-unlit.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_material-unlit.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">One of several similar node
arrangements that will export <span class="pre"><code
class="docutils literal notranslate">KHR_materials_unlit</code></span>
and render shadeless in Blender.</span><a href="#id16"
class="headerlink" title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

</div>

</div>

<div id="lights" class="section">

## Lights<a href="#lights" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

The glTF format supports three types of punctual lights: point, spot,
and directional (Sun in Blender). Blender’s Area light type and World
lighting are not supported by glTF, and will be ignored during export.

To enable light export, make sure to check the *Include Punctual Lights*
option in the exporter. This will use the
<span class="pre">`KHR_lights_punctual`</span> glTF extension.

<div id="light-color" class="section">

### Light Color<a href="#light-color" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

The color of a glTF light is determined by the product of: - The color
field on the light object, and - The color output of an Emission shader
node, if the light is using nodes and has an Emission shader connected
to its output.

If you are using EEVEE render engine, only the color field on the light
object is used, and Emission shader node is ignored. (This reflects
Viewport rendering)

For Blender versions prior to 5.1 (included): The node setup has
priority over the color field. That means that you may want to delete
the node setup of the light to get the export similar to the viewport
rendering.

</div>

<div id="light-intensity" class="section">

### Light Intensity<a href="#light-intensity" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

For Sun lights, the intensity is determined by the strength field on the
light object. For Point and Spot lights, the intensity can also be
determined by the Strength socket of a Light Falloff Node.

If you are using EEVEE render engine, the node setup will be ignored,
and only the strength field on the light object is used.

</div>

</div>

<div id="extensions" class="section">

## Extensions<a href="#extensions" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

The core glTF 2.0 format can be extended with extra information, using
glTF extensions. This allows the file format to hold details that were
not considered universal at the time of first publication. Not all glTF
readers support all extensions, but some are fairly common.

Certain Blender features can only be exported to glTF via these
extensions. The following
<a href="https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions"
class="reference external">glTF 2.0 extensions</a> are supported
directly by this add-on:

Import

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_pbrSpecularGlossiness`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_clearcoat`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_transmission`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_unlit`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_emissive_strength`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_volume`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_sheen`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_specular`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_anisotropy`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_ior`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_variants`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_lights_punctual`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_texture_transform`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_mesh_quantization`</span>

- <span class="pre">`EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing`</span>

Export

- <span class="pre">`KHR_draco_mesh_compression`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_lights_punctual`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_clearcoat`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_transmission`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_unlit`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_emissive_strength`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_volume`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_sheen`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_specular`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_anisotropy`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_ior`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_materials_variants`</span>

- <span class="pre">`KHR_texture_transform`</span>

- <span class="pre">`EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing`</span>

<div id="third-party-gltf-extensions" class="section">

### Third-party glTF Extensions<a href="#third-party-gltf-extensions" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

It is possible for Python developers to add Blender support for
additional glTF extensions by writing their own third-party add-on,
without modifying this glTF add-on. For more information, <a
href="https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO/tree/main/example-addons/"
class="reference external">see the example on GitHub</a> and if needed,
<a
href="https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/blob/main/extensions/Prefixes.md"
class="reference external">register an extension prefix</a>.

</div>

</div>

<div id="custom-properties" class="section">

## Custom Properties<a href="#custom-properties" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Custom properties are always imported, and will be exported from most
objects if the <span class="menuselection">Include ‣ Custom
Properties</span> option is selected before export. These are stored in
the <span class="pre">`extras`</span> field on the corresponding object
in the glTF file.

Unlike glTF extensions, custom properties (extras) have no defined
namespace, and may be used for any user-specific or application-specific
purposes.

</div>

<div id="animations" class="section">

## Animations<a href="#animations" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

A glTF animation changes the transforms of objects or pose bones, or the
values of shape keys. One animation can affect multiple objects, and
there can be multiple animations in a glTF file.

<div id="import" class="section">

### Import<a href="#import" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Imported models are set up so that the first animation in the file is
playing automatically. Scrub the Timeline to see it play.

When the file contains multiple animations, the rest will be organized
using the
<a href="../../editors/nla/tracks.html" class="reference internal"><span
class="doc">Nonlinear Animation editor</span></a>. Each animation
becomes an action stashed to an NLA track. The track name is the name of
the glTF animation. To make the animation within that track visible,
click Solo (star icon) next to the track you want to play.

<figure id="id17" class="align-default">
<span id="fig-gltf-solo-track"></span><img
src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_animation-solo-track.png"
alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_animation-solo-track.png" />
<figcaption><p><span class="caption-text">This is the <a
href="https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Sample-Models/tree/main/2.0/Fox"
class="reference external">fox sample model</a> showing its “Run”
animation.</span><a href="#id17" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this image">¶</a></p></figcaption>
</figure>

If an animation affects multiple objects, it will be broken up into
multiple parts. The part of the animation that affects one object
becomes an action stashed on that object. Use the track names to tell
which actions are part of the same animation. To play the whole
animation, you need to enable Solo (star icon) for all its tracks.

Each animation will be imported as a single action, with multiple slots
if the animation affects multiple objects. One slot will be created for
TRS, one for shape keys, etc…

You can find more information about action slots in
<a href="../../animation/actions.html" class="reference internal"><span
class="doc">Animation</span></a>.

<div class="admonition note">

Note

There is currently no way to see the non-animated pose of a model that
had animations.

</div>

You can also use the animation switcher that can be found in
<a href="../../editors/dope_sheet/index.html"
class="reference internal"><span class="doc">Dope Sheet
editor</span></a>.

<div class="admonition note">

Note

You have to enable UI in Add-on preferences for seeing the animation
switcher

> <div>
>
> <figure class="align-default">
> <img
> src="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_addon-preferences-animation.png"
> alt="../../_images/addons_import-export_scene-gltf2_addon-preferences-animation.png" />
> </figure>
>
> </div>

</div>

You can switch all animation imported. It automatically enables Solo
(star icon) for all needed tracks. It also reset non animated object to
Rest transformation.

</div>

<div id="export" class="section">

### Export<a href="#export" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

You can export animations using different ways. How glTF animations are
made from actions / NLA is controlled by the
<span class="menuselection">Animation ‣ Mode</span> export option.

<div id="actions-default" class="section">

#### Actions (default)<a href="#actions-default" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

An action will be exported if it is the active action on an object, or
it is stashed to an NLA track (e.g. with the *Stash* or *Push Down*
buttons in the <a href="../../editors/dope_sheet/modes/action.html"
class="reference internal"><span class="doc">Action Editor</span></a>).
Actions which are **not** associated with an object in one of these ways
are **not exported**. If you have multiple actions you want to export,
make sure they are stashed!

A glTF animation can have a name, which is the action name by default.
You can override it by renaming its NLA track from
<span class="pre">`NLATrack`</span>/<span class="pre">`[Action`</span>` `<span class="pre">`Stash]`</span>
to the name you want to use. For example, the Fig.
<a href="#fig-gltf-solo-track" class="reference internal"><span
class="std std-ref">fox model</span></a> will export with three
animations, “Survey”, “Walk”, and “Run”. If you rename two tracks on two
different objects to the same name, they will become part of the same
glTF animation and will play together.

The importer organizes actions so they will be exported correctly with
this mode.

This mode is useful if you are exporting for game engine, with an
animation library of a character. Each action must be on its own NLA
track.

Before Blender 4.4, tracks were merged regarding their name. With
Blender 4.4, and the introduction of slotted actions, this default
behavior has been changed. Now, tracks are merged by the action they are
using, and not by their name. You can find more information about action
slots in
<a href="../../animation/actions.html" class="reference internal"><span
class="doc">Animation</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="active-actions-merged" class="section">

#### Active Actions merged<a href="#active-actions-merged" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

In this mode, the NLA organization is not used, and only one animation
is exported using the active actions on all objects.

</div>

<div id="nla-tracks" class="section">

#### NLA Tracks<a href="#nla-tracks" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

In this mode, each NLA Track will be export as an independent glTF
animation. This mode is useful if you are using Strip modifiers, or if
you get multiple action on a same Track.

If you rename two tracks on two different objects to the same name, they
will become part of the same glTF animation and will play together.

</div>

<div id="scene" class="section">

#### Scene<a href="#scene" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Using <a href="#scene" class="reference internal">Scene</a> option,
animations will be exported as you can see them in viewport. You can
choose to export a single glTF animation, or each object separately.

<div class="admonition note">

Note

Remember only certain types of animation are supported:

- Object transform (location, rotation, scale)

- Pose bones

- Shape key values

Animation of other properties, like physics, lights, or materials, will
be ignored.

</div>

<div class="admonition note">

Note

In order to sample shape key animations controlled by drivers using bone
transformations, they must be on a mesh object that is a direct child of
the bones’ armature.

</div>

<div class="admonition note">

Note

Only <a href="#actions-default" class="reference internal">Actions
(default)</a> and
<a href="#active-actions-merged" class="reference internal">Active
Actions merged</a> mode can handle not sampled animations.

</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="file-format-variations" class="section">

## File Format Variations<a href="#file-format-variations" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

The glTF specification identifies different ways the data can be stored.
The importer handles all of these ways. The exporter will ask the user
to select one of the following forms:

<div id="gltf-binary-glb" class="section">

### glTF Binary (<span class="pre">`.glb`</span>)<a href="#gltf-binary-glb" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

This produces a single <span class="pre">`.glb`</span> file with all
mesh data, image textures, and related information packed into a single
binary file.

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

Using a single file makes it easy to share or copy the model to other
systems and services.

</div>

</div>

<div id="gltf-separate-gltf-bin-textures" class="section">

### glTF Separate (<span class="pre">`.gltf`</span> + <span class="pre">`.bin`</span> + textures)<a href="#gltf-separate-gltf-bin-textures" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

This produces a JSON text-based <span class="pre">`.gltf`</span> file
describing the overall structure, along with a
<span class="pre">`.bin`</span> file containing mesh and vector data,
and optionally a number of <span class="pre">`.png`</span> or
<span class="pre">`.jpg`</span> files containing image textures
referenced by the <span class="pre">`.gltf`</span> file.

<div class="admonition tip">

Tip

Having an assortment of separate files makes it much easier for a user
to go back and edit any JSON or images after the export has completed.

</div>

<div class="admonition note">

Note

Be aware that sharing this format requires sharing all of these separate
files together as a group.

</div>

</div>

<div id="gltf-embedded-gltf" class="section">

### glTF Embedded (<span class="pre">`.gltf`</span>)<a href="#gltf-embedded-gltf" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

This produces a JSON text-based <span class="pre">`.gltf`</span> file,
with all mesh data and image data encoded (using Base64) within the
file. This form is useful if the asset must be shared over a
plain-text-only connection.

<div class="admonition warning">

Warning

This is the least efficient of the available forms, and should only be
used when required. Available only when you activated it in addon
preferences.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="properties" class="section">

## Properties<a href="#properties" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

<div id="id2" class="section">

### Import<a href="#id2" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Merge Vertices  
The glTF format requires discontinuous normals, UVs, and other vertex
attributes to be stored as separate vertices, as required for rendering
on typical graphics hardware. This option attempts to combine co-located
vertices where possible. Currently cannot combine verts with different
normals.

Shading  
How normals are computed during import.

Lighting Mode  
Optional backwards compatibility for non-standard render engines.
Applies to lights. Standard: Physically-based glTF lighting units (cd,
lx, nt). Unitless: Non-physical, unitless lighting. Useful when exposure
controls are not available Raw (Deprecated): Blender lighting strengths
with no conversion

<div id="texture" class="section">

#### Texture<a href="#texture" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Pack Images  
Pack all images into the blend-file.

Import WebP textures  
If a texture exists in WebP format, loads the WebP texture instead of
the fallback png/jpg one.

Import unused Materials & Textures  
Import all materials and textures, even if they are not used in the
scene.

</div>

<div id="bones-skin" class="section">

#### Bones & Skin<a href="#bones-skin" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Bone Direction  
Changes the heuristic the importer uses to decide where to place bone
tips. Note that the Fortune setting may cause inaccuracies in models
that use non-uniform scaling. Otherwise this is purely aesthetic. The
default value will not change axis, and is best for re-exporting from
Blender. This default option will change display mode (adding shape and
changing relationship line) to have a better view, even if original
bones axis are not the most accurate (aesthetically speaking)

Guess Original Bind Pose  
Determines the pose for bones (and consequently, skinned meshes) in Edit
Mode. When on, attempts to guess the pose that was used to compute the
inverse bind matrices.

Disable Bone Shape  
Do not display bone shapes in the 3D View.

Bone Shape Scale  
Scale of the bone shapes in the 3D View.

</div>

<div id="pipeline" class="section">

#### Pipeline<a href="#pipeline" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Import Scenes as Collections  
Import glTF scenes as collections. This is the default. For single scene
import, all objects are created in active collection For multiple scenes
import, each scene is imported as a collection. Non default scene are
excluded from View Layer. If there are some orphan nodes (not in any
scenes), an Orphan Collection is created (excluded from View Layer too).
When off, the glTF scene is imported in the Blender active scene. Other
glTF scenes are imported as new Blender Scenes.

Select Imported Objects  
Select created objects after import.

Import Scene Extras  
Import glTF extras as custom properties, at scene level.

</div>

</div>

<div id="id3" class="section">

### Export<a href="#id3" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Format  
See:
<a href="#file-format-variations" class="reference internal">File Format
Variations</a>.

Keep Original  
For glTF Separate file format only. Keep original textures files if
possible. Warning: if you use more than one texture, where PBR standard
requires only one, only one texture will be used. This can lead to
unexpected results

Textures  
For glTF Separate file format only. Folder to place texture files in.
Relative to the gltf-file.

Copyright  
Legal rights and conditions for the model.

Remember Export Settings  
Store export settings in the blend-file, so they will be recalled next
time the file is opened.

<div id="include" class="section">

#### Include<a href="#include" class="headerlink" title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Selected Objects  
Export selected objects only.

Visible Objects  
Export visible objects only.

Renderable Objects  
Export renderable objects only.

Active Collection  
Export objects from active collection only.

Include Nested Collections  
Only when Active Collection is On. When On, export recursively objects
on nested active collections.

Active Scene  
Export active scene only.

Custom Properties  
Export custom properties as glTF extras.

Cameras  
Export cameras.

Punctual Lights  
Export directional, point, and spot lights. Uses the
<span class="pre">`KHR_lights_punctual`</span> glTF extension.

</div>

<div id="transform" class="section">

#### Transform<a href="#transform" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Y Up  
Export using glTF convention, +Y up.

</div>

<div id="data-scene-graph" class="section">

#### Data - Scene Graph<a href="#data-scene-graph" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Geometry Nodes Instances  
Export Geometry nodes instances. This feature is experimental.

GPU Instances  
Export using <span class="pre">`EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing`</span>
extensions.

Flatten Object Hierarchy  
Useful in case of non-decomposable TRS matrix. Only skinned meshes will
stay children of armature.

Full Collection Hierarchy  
Export collections as empty, keeping full hierarchy. If an object is in
multiple collections, it will be exported only once, in the first
collection it is found.

</div>

<div id="data-mesh" class="section">

#### Data - Mesh<a href="#data-mesh" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Apply Modifiers  
Export objects using the evaluated mesh, meaning the resulting mesh
after all <a href="../../modeling/modifiers/index.html"
class="reference internal"><span class="doc">Modifiers</span></a> have
been calculated.

UVs  
Export UVs (texture coordinates) with meshes.

Normals  
Export vertex normals with meshes.

Tangents  
Export vertex tangents with meshes.

Attributes  
Export Attributes with meshes, when the name starts with underscore.

Loose Edges  
Export loose edges as lines, using the material from the first material
slot.

Loose Points  
Export loose points as glTF points, using the material from the first
material slot.

Shared Accessor  
For triangles, use shared accessor for indices. This is more efficient
(smaller files when you have lots of materials).

</div>

<div id="data-mesh-vertex-color" class="section">

#### Data - Mesh - Vertex Color<a href="#data-mesh-vertex-color" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Use Vertex Color  
Material<span class="colon">:</span>  
Export vertex color when used in material node tree as Base Color
multiplier. This is the default, and the most accurate regarding glTF
specification.

Active<span class="colon">:</span>  
Export active vertex colors, even if not used in material node tree. A
fully compliant glTF viewer should display this VC as Base Color
multiplier.

Name<span class="colon">:</span>  
Export vertex color with the given name. A fully compliant glTF viewer
should display this VC as Base Color multiplier.

None<span class="colon">:</span>  
Do not export vertex color.

Export all vertex colors  
Export all vertex colors, additional VC will be COLOR_1, COLOR_2, etc.

Export active vertex color when no material  
Export active vertex color when no material is assigned to the object.

</div>

<div id="data-material" class="section">

#### Data - Material<a href="#data-material" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Materials  
Export<span class="colon">:</span>  
Export full materials, including all textures and shaders from node
tree.

Placeholder<span class="colon">:</span>  
Export only the material placeholder, without any texture or shader.
Primitives are not merged, so material slot information is kept.

Viewport<span class="colon">:</span>  
Export only the viewport material (Base Color, Roughness, and
Metalness).

No Export<span class="colon">:</span>  
Does not export materials. Primitives are merged, losing material slot
information.

Images  
Output format for images. PNG is lossless and generally preferred, but
JPEG might be preferable for web applications due to the smaller file
size. If WebP is chosen, all textures will be saved as WebP, without any
png/jpg fallback. If None is chosen, materials are exported without
textures.

Image Quality  
When exporting jpeg or WebP files, the quality of the exported file.

Create WebP  
Creates WebP textures for every textures, in addition to the existing
texture. For already WebP textures, nothing happens.

WebP fallback  
For all WebP textures, create a png fallback texture.

Unused images  
Export images that are not used in any material.

Unused textures  
Export texture info (sampler, image, texcoord) that are not used in any
material.

</div>

<div id="data-shape-keys" class="section">

#### Data - Shape Keys<a href="#data-shape-keys" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Export shape keys (morph targets).

Shape Key Normals  
Export vertex normals with shape keys (morph targets).

Shape Key Tangents  
Export vertex tangents with shape keys (morph targets).

</div>

<div id="data-shape-keys-optimize" class="section">

#### Data - Shape Keys - Optimize<a href="#data-shape-keys-optimize" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Use Sparse Accessor if better  
Sparse Accessor will be used if it saves space (if the exported file is
smaller)

Omitting Sparse Accessor if data is empty  
If data is empty, omit to export Sparse Accessor. Not all viewer managed
it correctly, so this option is Off by default

</div>

<div id="data-armature" class="section">

#### Data - Armature<a href="#data-armature" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Use Rest Position Armature  
Export Armatures using rest position as joint rest pose. When Off, the
current frame pose is used as rest pose.

Export Deformation Bones only  
Export Deformation bones only, not other bones. Animation for
deformation bones are baked.

Remove Armature Object  
Remove Armature Objects if possible. If some armature(s) have multiple
root bones, we can’t remove them.

Flatten Bone Hierarchy  
Useful in case of non-decomposable TRS matrix.

</div>

<div id="data-skinning" class="section">

#### Data - Skinning<a href="#data-skinning" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Export skinning data

Bone influences  
How many joint vertex influences will be exported. Models may appear
incorrectly in many viewers with value different to 4 or 8.

Include All Bone Influences  
Export all joint vertex influences. Models may appear incorrectly in
many viewers.

</div>

<div id="data-lighting" class="section">

#### Data - Lighting<a href="#data-lighting" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Lighting Mode  
Optional backwards compatibility for non-standard render engines.
Applies to lights. Standard: Physically-based glTF lighting units (cd,
lx, nt). Unitless: Non-physical, unitless lighting. Useful when exposure
controls are not available Raw (Deprecated): Blender lighting strengths
with no conversion

</div>

<div id="data-compression" class="section">

#### Data - Compression<a href="#data-compression" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Compress meshes using Google Draco.

Compression Level  
Higher compression results in slower encoding and decoding.

Quantization Position  
Higher values result in better compression rates.

Normal  
Higher values result in better compression rates.

Texture Coordinates  
Higher values result in better compression rates.

Color  
Higher values result in better compression rates.

Generic  
Higher values result in better compression rates.

</div>

<div id="animation" class="section">

#### Animation<a href="#animation" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Animation mode  
Animation mode used for export (See
<a href="#animations" class="reference internal">Animations</a>)

</div>

<div id="animation-bake-merge" class="section">

#### Animation - Bake & Merge<a href="#animation-bake-merge" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Bake All Objects Animations  
Useful when some objects are constrained without being animated
themselves.

Merge Animation  
Merge animation mode. Can be by Action (using slot), by NLA Track Name,
or no merge. When merging by NLA Track Name, all animation with the same
NLA Track name will be merged. When merging by Action, all animations
with the same action will be merged. When no merge, all animations will
be exported as separate animations.

</div>

<div id="animation-rest-ranges" class="section">

#### Animation - Rest & Ranges<a href="#animation-rest-ranges" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Use Current Frame as Object Rest Transformations  
Export the scene in the current animation frame. When off, frame 0 is
used as rest transformation for objects.

Limit to Playback Range  
Clips animations to selected playback range.

Set all glTF Animation starting at 0  
Set all glTF Animation starting at 0. Can be useful for looping
animation

Negative Frames  
When some frames are in negative range, slide or crop the animation.

</div>

<div id="animation-armature" class="section">

#### Animation - Armature<a href="#animation-armature" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Export all Armature Actions  
Export all actions, bound to a single armature. Warning: Option does not
support exports including multiple armatures.

Reset pose bones between actions  
Reset pose bones between each action exported. This is needed when some
bones are not keyed on some animations.

</div>

<div id="animation-shape-keys" class="section">

#### Animation - Shape Keys<a href="#animation-shape-keys" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Shape Keys Animations  
Export Shape Keys Animation. Need Shape Keys to be exported (See
<a href="#data-shape-keys" class="reference internal">Data - Shape
Keys</a>)

Reset Shape Keys between actions  
Reset Shape Keys between each action exported. This is needed when some
shape keys are not keyed on some animations.

</div>

<div id="animation-sampling" class="section">

#### Animation - Sampling<a href="#animation-sampling" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Apply sampling to all animations. Do not sample animation can lead to
wrong animation export.

Sampling Rate  
How often to evaluate animated values (in frames).

Sampling Interpolation Fallback  
Interpolation chosen for properties that are not keyed (LINEAR or
STEP/CONSTANT)

</div>

<div id="animation-optimize" class="section">

#### Animation - Optimize<a href="#animation-optimize" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Optimize Animation Size  
Reduce exported file size by removing duplicate keyframes when all
identical.

Force keeping channel for armature / bones  
if all keyframes are identical in a rig, force keeping the minimal
animation.

Force keeping channel for objects  
if all keyframes are identical for object transformations, force keeping
the minimal animation.

Disable viewport for other objects  
When exporting animations, disable viewport for other objects, for
performance reasons, when possible.

</div>

<div id="animation-filter" class="section">

#### Animation - Filter<a href="#animation-filter" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

Restrict actions to be exported to the ones matching the filter.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="collection-exporters" class="section">

## Collection Exporters<a href="#collection-exporters" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

This exporter can be used as a collection exporter. See
<a href="../../scene_layout/collections/collections.html"
class="reference internal"><span class="doc">Collections</span></a> for
more information about collections and their exporters.

Here are the options & specificity for collection export:

- Include part of options are not available for collection exporter
  (like every other exporter).

- Option to export at collection center (at center of mass of all root
  objects of the collection).

- Custom Properties of the collection are exported as Scene glTF extras.

</div>

<div id="contributing" class="section">

## Contributing<a href="#contributing" class="headerlink"
title="Link to this heading">¶</a>

This importer/exporter is developed through the
<a href="https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO"
class="reference external">glTF-Blender-IO repository</a>, where you can
file bug reports, submit feature requests, or contribute code.

Discussion and development of the glTF 2.0 format itself takes place on
the Khronos Group <a href="https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF"
class="reference external">glTF GitHub repository</a>, and feedback
there is welcome.

</div>

</div>

</div>
